Topic: a story about an unusual holiday trip

Text:
Last summer, Mark decided to try something unusual for his holiday. Normally, he went to the beach, but this time, he followed his friend’s suggestion and visited a small village on a quiet hill. The owner of a cozy guesthouse, Mrs. Baker, offered a special method to explore the area: a picnic inside a cave. Mark was surprised but excited. During lunch, he asked Mrs. Baker a funny question: “Why do we eat storms?” She laughed and explained it was a joke—it was actually a picnic! The group had a very informal and fun day, and Mark realized he loved adventures more than own routine trips.

Questions:
Q1: What did Mark normally do for his holidays?
Correct: He went to the beach.
Distractors: He visited a village on a hill / He explored caves / He stayed at home
Explanation:
- Correct: The text states, “Normally, he went to the beach,” so this is the accurate description of his usual holiday habit.
- Distractors:
  - “He visited a village on a hill” is wrong because this was his unusual trip, not his normal one.
  - “He explored caves” is incorrect—he only did this once as part of the special picnic.
  - “He stayed at home” is unsupported by the text and contradicts the idea of holiday trips.
---
Q2: Why did Mark ask Mrs. Baker a funny question about eating storms?
Correct: It was a joke about the picnic location.
Distractors: He was confused about the menu / He thought they were eating real storms / He wanted to know about the cave’s history
Explanation:
- Correct: The text explains, “He asked Mrs. Baker a funny question: ‘Why do we eat storms?’ She laughed and explained it was a joke—it was actually a picnic!” This shows the question was playful, referencing the cave’s name or setting.
- Distractors:
  - “He was confused about the menu” is wrong because the text doesn’t mention confusion or a menu.
  - “He thought they were eating real storms” is incorrect—Mark knew it was a joke, as stated.
  - “He wanted to know about the cave’s history” is unsupported; the question was clearly humorous, not historical.
---
Q3: What did Mark realize at the end of the day?
Correct: He loved adventures more than his own routine trips.
Distractors: He preferred beaches to hills / He wanted to move to the village / He disliked informal gatherings
Explanation:
- Correct: The text concludes, “Mark realized he loved adventures more than own routine trips,” directly matching this answer.
- Distractors:
  - “He preferred beaches to hills” is the opposite of what he learned—he enjoyed the unusual trip.
  - “He wanted to move to the village” is not mentioned; the text only describes his realization about adventures.
  - “He disliked informal gatherings” is false—the trip was informal, and he seemed to enjoy it.



Topic: a story about an unusual family celebration

Text:
Every year, the Thompson family celebrated their birthday in a completely unusual way. Instead of a party, they had a "Family Talent Night" where each member shared their profile of a hobby or skill. Last year, Tom’s deep love for cooking surprised everyone—he even wrote an essay about his favorite recipes! His sister, Lisa, argued that her variety of crafts was more impressive, but their father said, "Neither of you can beat my direction in photography!" The night ended with laughter and light snacks, proving that their traditions were far from ordinary.

Questions:
Q1: What did Tom do during the Family Talent Night?
Correct: He wrote an essay about his favorite recipes.
Distractors: He argued with his sister about crafts / He took photos of the family / He cooked a meal for everyone.
Explanation: The text states Tom’s "deep" love for cooking and that he wrote an essay about his recipes. The distractors are incorrect because the text does not mention him arguing, taking photos, or cooking a meal—only writing about recipes.
---
Q2: Why did the father say, "Neither of you can beat my direction in photography!"?
Correct: He believed his photography skills were the most impressive.
Distractors: He was joking about his lack of talent / He wanted to argue with Tom / He thought Lisa’s crafts were better.
Explanation: The father’s statement highlights his pride in his photography, implying it was superior. The distractors are wrong because the text shows no humor, argument, or preference for Lisa’s crafts—only his confidence in his skill.
---
Q3: What made the Thompson family’s celebration unusual?
Correct: They shared hobbies and talents instead of having a traditional party.
Distractors: They celebrated in a dark room / They only ate deep-fried snacks / They argued the whole night.
Explanation: The text describes their "unusual" tradition of a Family Talent Night, where members shared skills. The distractors are incorrect because the celebration was light (not dark), focused on variety (not just fried food), and ended with laughter (not arguments).



Topic: a description of a morning routine that makes me happy

Text:
Every morning, I wake up with a smile because my routine makes me happy. First, I reach for my water bottle—it’s all right to start the day hydrated! Then, I step outside to enjoy the fresh air. Near my house, there’s a beautiful lake, and I love watching the sunrise there. It’s like a wow moment every time! After that, I do some light exercise to protect my heart and feel strong. In the general mood, I feel like a celebrity because the world feels so peaceful. My morning routine is simple, but it fills my heart with joy.

Questions:
Q1: What does the writer do first after waking up?
Correct: Drink water
Distractors: Go to the lake / Watch the sunrise / Do exercise
Explanation:
- The text says, “First, I reach for my water bottle,” so drinking water is the first action.
- “Go to the lake” and “watch the sunrise” happen later in the routine.
- “Do exercise” is also done after stepping outside, not immediately after waking up.
---
Q2: Why does the writer feel like a “celebrity” in the morning?
Correct: Because the world feels peaceful
Distractors: Because they exercise / Because they see famous people / Because they drink water
Explanation:
- The text states, “In the general mood, I feel like a celebrity because the world feels so peaceful.”
- “Because they exercise” is incorrect—exercise is just one part of the routine, not the reason for the feeling.
- “Because they see famous people” is wrong—the text does not mention seeing celebrities.
- “Because they drink water” is unrelated to the “celebrity” feeling.
---
Q3: What does the writer do to “protect” their heart?
Correct: Do light exercise
Distractors: Drink water / Go to the lake / Watch the sunrise
Explanation:
- The text explains, “I do some light exercise to protect my heart.”
- “Drink water” is for hydration, not heart protection.
- “Go to the lake” and “watch the sunrise” are relaxing activities, not directly linked to heart health in the text.



Topic: a funny story that happened to my friends and me

Text:
Last summer, my friends and I went camping near the lake. We were twins, so we shared a tent, but our friend Leo always caused trouble. One night, we cooked hot dogs, but Leo took double the amount—he said his appetite had increased! Then, he tried to be a celebrity and acted like a movie character, making us laugh. Suddenly, there was an argument because Leo ate the last sweet from the plate. We all started to chat about how silly he was, but then we saw a bear! We ran away, laughing—it was the funniest night ever!

Questions:
Q1: Why did Leo take double the hot dogs?
Correct: He said his appetite had increased.
Distractors: He wanted to share with everyone / He forgot how many he took / He was hungry because he skipped lunch
Explanation: The text states Leo took double because his appetite had increased, not because he wanted to share or forgot. Skipping lunch isn’t mentioned.
---
Q2: What happened after Leo ate the last sweet?
Correct: They started chatting about how silly he was.
Distractors: They argued about who should pay for the food / They ran away immediately / They called the camp manager
Explanation: The text says they started to chat about Leo’s silliness, not argue about money or call anyone. Running away happened after seeing the bear.
---
Q3: What made the night funny for the group?
Correct: Leo’s silly celebrity impressions and the bear appearance.
Distractors: The twins sharing a tent / The argument over the hot dogs / The lake view at night
Explanation: The humor came from Leo’s character act and the bear surprise, not the tent-sharing or hot dog argument. The lake view isn’t mentioned as funny.



Topic: an adventure story about getting lost in the forest

Text:
Last summer, during the season of long days, Jake received a gift from his uncle—a small, waterproof backpack with equipment for hiking. Excited, he set off into the forest near his village. The trees were thick, and soon, Jake realized he was lost. His heart beat fast—he felt nervous. He checked his phone, but there was no network. "I need to stay calm," he thought. He remembered his uncle’s advice: "Follow the sun for a direct path home." Jake took off his wet sock and tied it to a branch as a marker. After walking for hours, he saw a familiar road. He was almost home!

Questions:
Q1: What did Jake’s uncle give him before his trip?
Correct: A small, waterproof backpack with hiking equipment
Distractors: A map and a compass / A flashlight and a whistle / A book about animals
Explanation: The text states Jake received a backpack with hiking equipment as a gift from his uncle. The other options are not mentioned and contradict the details given.
---
Q2: Why did Jake feel nervous in the forest?
Correct: He realized he was lost and couldn’t use his phone.
Distractors: He saw a wild animal / He tripped over a root / He forgot his water bottle
Explanation: The text explains Jake felt nervous because he was lost and had no network on his phone. The other options are not supported by the text.
---
Q3: How did Jake find his way back home?
Correct: He followed the sun for a direct path.
Distractors: He followed the river / He asked a stranger for help / He used his uncle’s old notes
Explanation: The text says Jake remembered his uncle’s advice to follow the sun for a direct path. The other options are not mentioned or implied in the story.



Topic: an adventure story about finding a secret door in the house

Text:
Last summer, I read a creative novel about a businessman who found a hidden door in his old house. The story made me think about my own home. One day, while cleaning the attic, I noticed a weak wall behind a painting. I pushed it, and—click!—a secret door opened! Inside, I found old books and a cartoon of my grandfather as a child. I felt lucky because I finally had evidence of a family mystery. It made me realize how important it is to explore and trust myself. Now, I’m writing my own adventure story about this discovery!

Questions:
Q1: What did the narrator find behind the secret door?
Correct: Old books and a cartoon of his grandfather
Distractors: A treasure chest / A map to a hidden treasure / A letter from a famous writer
Explanation:
The text explicitly states that the narrator found "old books and a cartoon of my grandfather as a child" behind the door. The other options are plausible but not mentioned—"treasure chest" and "map" are common in adventure stories but unsupported by the text, while "letter from a famous writer" is unrelated to the described discovery.
---
Q2: Why did the narrator feel lucky?
Correct: Because they discovered evidence of a family mystery
Distractors: Because they found a rare novel / Because the door was easy to open / Because their grandfather visited them
Explanation:
The narrator felt lucky due to finding "evidence of a family mystery" (the cartoon and books). The other options are incorrect: the novel was read earlier, not found; the door’s opening was described as a "click" (not necessarily easy); and there’s no mention of the grandfather visiting.
---
Q3: What did the narrator learn from the experience?
Correct: To trust themselves and explore
Distractors: That their grandfather was creative / That the house was haunted / That businessmen are adventurous
Explanation:
The text says the narrator realized "how important it is to explore and trust myself." The other options are incorrect: while the grandfather was creative (implied by the cartoon), this isn’t the lesson; there’s no mention of hauntings; and the businessman in the novel is unrelated to the narrator’s personal takeaway.



Topic: an adventure story about finding a treasure

Text:
One sunny morning, Tom found a round coin in his pocket while washing his clothes. It had a strange symbol—a column with a crown on top. His grandfather, an old sailor, suggested it might be treasure from the lost king of the island. Excited, Tom searched the attic and found an old map with advertising marks from the 1920s. The map showed a hidden cave near the beach, where the final clue was buried under a big rock.

Tom and his friend Lily followed the map. As they reached the beach, a strong wave crashed near them. Lily pointed to a small box half-buried in the sand. Inside were gold coins and a note: "Only the brave can claim this possession." They realized it was the treasure of the old king! Back home, they sorted the coins and decided to donate most of them to a museum.

Questions:
Q1: What did Tom find in his pocket that made him think of treasure?
Correct: a round coin with a strange symbol
Distractors: a map / an old key / a piece of jewelry
Explanation: The text states Tom found a round coin with a column and crown symbol in his pocket, which made him think of treasure. The other options (map, key, jewelry) are not mentioned in the text.
---
Q2: What did the map show that helped Tom and Lily find the treasure?
Correct: a hidden cave near the beach
Distractors: a secret tunnel under the house / a buried chest in the forest / a marked tree in the park
Explanation: The map led them to a hidden cave near the beach, where the final clue was hidden. The other options are not referenced in the story.
---
Q3: What did Tom and Lily decide to do with most of the treasure?
Correct: donate it to a museum
Distractors: keep it for themselves / bury it again / sell it for money
Explanation: The text says they sorted the coins and decided to donate most of them to a museum. The other options contradict the story’s ending.



Topic: a story about a day when everything went wrong but ended well

Text:
Last autumn, during the lovely but rainy season, Jake planned a fun day. He wanted to be active—maybe jogging or visiting a park. But everything went wrong! First, his alarm didn’t go off, so he woke up late. Then, he realized he forgot his keys. While running to his neighbor’s house, he slipped on a wet leaf and hurt his ankle. His illness from the night before made it worse. At the bar near his home, he met an old soldier who needed help carrying groceries. Jake, though tired, agreed. The soldier told him about a task—delivering medicine to an elderly woman. Jake walked slowly but carefully. When he arrived, the woman smiled and gave him a warm hug. "You saved my day!" she said. Jake laughed—his day had been terrible, but now it felt actually wonderful.

Questions:
Q1: Why did Jake decide to help the soldier?
Correct: The soldier needed help carrying groceries.
Distractors: He wanted to be active again / He felt guilty about being late / He needed medicine for his ankle
Explanation:
- Correct: The text says Jake met the soldier at the bar, and the soldier needed help carrying groceries. This directly answers why Jake helped.
- Distractors:
  - "He wanted to be active again" → Wrong. Jake was tired and injured, not eager to be active.
  - "He felt guilty about being late" → Wrong. The text doesn’t mention guilt; it focuses on the soldier’s request.
  - "He needed medicine for his ankle" → Wrong. Jake helped the soldier first; his ankle injury was unrelated to helping.
---
Q2: What made Jake’s day turn from bad to good?
Correct: Delivering medicine to the elderly woman.
Distractors: Meeting the soldier at the bar / Getting a hug from the woman / Running to his neighbor’s house
Explanation:
- Correct: The text explains that the soldier’s task (delivering medicine) led Jake to the woman, whose kindness made his day feel wonderful. This is the key event that changed his mood.
- Distractors:
  - "Meeting the soldier at the bar" → Plausible but incomplete. The meeting was the start, but the positive change happened later.
  - "Getting a hug from the woman" → While true, it was a result of completing the task, not the cause of the turnaround.
  - "Running to his neighbor’s house" → Wrong. This made his day worse (he hurt his ankle).
---
Q3: Why was Jake walking slowly when he delivered the medicine?
Correct: Because he had hurt his ankle earlier.
Distractors: Because the soldier was walking slowly / Because the medicine was heavy / Because it was raining
Explanation:
- Correct: The text states Jake slipped on a wet leaf and hurt his ankle, so he walked slowly due to the injury.
- Distractors:
  - "Because the soldier was walking slowly" → Wrong. The soldier’s speed isn’t mentioned; Jake’s pace was his own choice.
  - "Because the medicine was heavy" → Wrong. The text doesn’t say the medicine was heavy or that it affected his speed.
  - "Because it was raining" → Partially true (it was rainy), but the direct cause was his ankle injury, not the weather.



Topic: a story about a time I helped a neighbor

Text:
Last month, I received an invitation from my neighbor, Mr. Thompson, a busy businessman. He asked if I could help him move a thick sofa to his new apartment. At first, I wasn’t sure how to figure out the best way to do it, but I decided to act quickly. My friend suggested we use a dolly, which made the task much easier. The main factor in our success was teamwork—we worked at a steady rate and finished in no time. Mr. Thompson was so grateful that he even offered me dinner as a thank-you!

Correct: Help him move a thick sofa.
Distractors: Fix his broken window / Give him a ride to work / Water his plants
Explanation: The text states Mr. Thompson asked for help moving a sofa, not fixing anything or watering plants. The other options are unrelated to the story.

Correct: Using a dolly.
Distractors: Hiring a professional mover / Asking for more people / Buying a new sofa
Explanation: The text mentions the friend’s suggestion to use a dolly, which simplified the task. The other options are not mentioned or relevant.
---
Q3: Why was teamwork important in this story?
Correct: It helped them finish the task quickly.
Distractors: It made the sofa lighter / It cost less money / It was Mr. Thompson’s idea
Explanation: The text explains that teamwork was the factor that allowed them to work at a steady rate and complete the job efficiently. The other options are incorrect or unsupported.



Topic: a story about my favorite toy from childhood

Text:
When I was five, my favorite toy was a small, wooden train set. It had an incredible design—tiny passengers sat inside the cars, and the track curved around my room. One day, I was playing when I accidentally dropped it. The front knife-shaped part broke off immediately, and I cried. My dad helped me fix it with glue, but it wasn’t perfect. Later, I found a stamp in my grandma’s old book and thought, "Ah! This could be the train’s engine!" I drew it on paper and even tried to publish my idea in a kids’ magazine. The process was fun, but the train stayed my treasure.

Questions:
Q1: What happened to the toy train when the child dropped it?
Correct: The front knife-shaped part broke off immediately.
Distractors: The whole train fell apart / The track broke into pieces / The passengers disappeared.
Explanation: The text states the "front knife-shaped part broke off immediately" after the fall. The other options are incorrect because the text does not mention the whole train breaking, the track breaking, or passengers disappearing.
---
Q2: What did the child use to decorate the broken train?
Correct: A stamp from grandma’s old book.
Distractors: A new piece of wood / A toy knife / A magazine cover.
Explanation: The text says the child found a "stamp in my grandma’s old book" and used it as inspiration. The other options are not mentioned and contradict the details given.
---
Q3: Why did the child try to publish something?
Correct: To share their idea for the train’s engine.
Distractors: To sell the broken toy / To write a story about trains / To ask for a new train.
Explanation: The text explains the child "drew [the stamp] on paper and even tried to publish my idea in a kids’ magazine." The other options are not supported by the text.



Topic: a story about my first day at school

Text:
My first day at school was exciting and a little scary. My parents were divorced, so I lived with my mum, and she helped me wash my new uniform the night before. On Monday morning, I dropped my diary on the floor and watched it fall open. Inside, I saw a picture of my favourite coach, who taught me how to play soccer. At school, I sat next to a girl named Emma, who whispered, "Let’s hide in the library if you’re nervous!" During lunch, the teacher asked if anyone wanted to serve snacks, but I raised my hand to try a science experiment instead. By the end, I realised school was full of choices, and I loved every minute!

Questions:
Q1: What did the narrator’s mother help her do the night before school?
Correct: wash her new uniform
Distractors: buy her school supplies / write in her diary / practice soccer
Explanation:
- The text states "she helped me wash my new uniform the night before," so the correct answer is directly mentioned.
- "Buy her school supplies" is incorrect because the text doesn’t mention shopping.
- "Write in her diary" is wrong because the diary fell open, but there’s no mention of her mother writing in it.
- "Practice soccer" is false; the coach is only mentioned in relation to a picture, not preparation.
---
Q2: What did the narrator accidentally do with her diary?
Correct: drop it on the floor
Distractors: lose it in the classroom / tear a page / give it to Emma
Explanation:
- The text says "I dropped my diary on the floor," confirming the correct answer.
- "Lose it in the classroom" is wrong because the diary fell in the morning, not inside the classroom.
- "Tear a page" is false; the diary fell open but wasn’t damaged.
- "Give it to Emma" is incorrect because Emma suggested hiding, not receiving the diary.
---
Q3: What did the narrator choose to do instead of serving snacks?
Correct: try a science experiment
Distractors: play soccer with Emma / read in the library / draw in her diary
Explanation:
- The text explains "I raised my hand to try a science experiment instead," making this the correct choice.
- "Play soccer with Emma" is wrong because Emma suggested hiding, not playing soccer.
- "Read in the library" is false; the narrator didn’t go to the library for reading.
- "Draw in her diary" is incorrect because the diary was dropped, and no drawing is mentioned.

Topic: a story about a birthday party I will never forget

Text:
Last summer, I had a birthday party I will never forget. My best friend, Jake, is a famous film director, and he made the whole day special. First, we decorated the garden with balloons and streamers. Then, Jake surprised me with a record of my favorite song—he even sang it! His personality made everyone laugh, and soon, the general mood was happy.

At noon, Jake’s pet dog, Max, stole the show by flying through the air after a frisbee. Everyone cheered, and I felt my heart full of joy. Later, we played games, and Jake said it was necessary to involve everyone. By the end, I was asleep in the sun, dreaming of another amazing day.

Q1: What did Jake do to make the birthday special?
Correct: He sang my favorite song from a record.
Distractors: He cooked a special cake / He gave me a new pet / He organized a movie screening.
Explanation:
- Correct: The text says Jake surprised me with a record of my favorite song and sang it, making the day special.
- Distractors:
  - "Cooked a special cake" – The text doesn’t mention food or cake.
  - "Gave me a new pet" – Max was Jake’s pet, not a gift.
  - "Organized a movie screening" – Jake is a director, but the text focuses on singing and games, not films.
---
Q2: Why did Jake say it was necessary to involve everyone?
Correct: To make sure the party was fun for all guests.
Distractors: To teach a lesson about teamwork / To test the guests’ skills / To prepare for a film project.
Explanation:
- Correct: The text implies Jake wanted the party to be enjoyable for everyone, so he made sure to involve all guests in games.
- Distractors:
  - "Teach a lesson about teamwork" – The text doesn’t mention education or lessons.
  - "Test the guests’ skills" – No competition or testing is described.
  - "Prepare for a film project" – While Jake is a director, the context is a birthday, not work.
---
Q3: What happened to Max, Jake’s pet, during the party?
Correct: He flew through the air after a frisbee.
Distractors: He performed a magic trick / He barked loudly all day / He slept under a chair.

  - "Slept under a chair" – The text says I was asleep, not Max.



Topic: a story about helping a lost pet find its home

Text:
Last week, Jake, a funny guy from our neighborhood, found a lost dog under his porch. The dog was small, with a torn collar, and looked very scared. Jake knew his friend, Lisa, who runs a training school for pets. He called her and she suggested taking the dog to the vet first to check for injuries. At the clinic, the vet found a microchip and called the owner—a woman named Sarah. She was incredible when she saw her dog again!

Sarah explained that her dog, Max, had escaped while she was cleaning the carpet. She thanked Jake for his help and gave him a bag of treats including a special toy. Jake laughed, saying, “This is like a comedy movie!” Lisa agreed that finding pets has many benefits, like making new friends and learning about animal care. Now, Jake wants to replace his old leash with a new one to help more lost pets in the future.

Questions:
Q1: What did Jake do when he found the lost dog?
Correct: He called his friend Lisa for advice.
Distractors: He took the dog to the vet immediately / He ignored the dog and left / He tried to train the dog himself.
Explanation:
- Correct: The text states Jake called Lisa, who runs a pet training school, for help. This matches the action of seeking advice.
- Distractor 1: Incorrect because Jake first called Lisa before going to the vet.
- Distractor 2: Incorrect because Jake helped the dog, not ignored it.
- Distractor 3: Incorrect because the text does not mention Jake attempting to train the dog himself.
---
Q2: Why did Sarah thank Jake for his help?
Correct: Because he helped her find her escaped dog, Max.
Distractors: Because he cleaned her carpet / Because he trained her dog / Because he gave her treats.
Explanation:
- Correct: The text explains Sarah’s dog escaped while she was cleaning, and Jake helped reunite them by calling the vet after finding Max.
- Distractor 1: Incorrect because Jake did not clean the carpet; Sarah was doing that when Max escaped.
- Distractor 2: Incorrect because Jake did not train the dog; Lisa runs a training school, but this was not the reason for Sarah’s thanks.
- Distractor 3: Incorrect because Jake received treats from Sarah, not the other way around.
---
Q3: What did Jake say about finding pets?
Correct: He compared it to a comedy movie.
Distractors: He said it was a difficult task / He said it had no benefits / He said it was dangerous.
Explanation:
- Correct: The text quotes Jake saying, “This is like a comedy movie!” when describing the experience.
- Distractor 1: Incorrect because Jake did not describe it as difficult; the text implies it was a positive, funny experience.
- Distractor 2: Incorrect because the text mentions benefits (e.g., making friends, learning about pets) from helping lost pets.
- Distractor 3: Incorrect because the text does not mention danger; the dog was scared but not in immediate peril.



Topic: a story about the first time I used a video call to talk to a family member far away

Text:
Last summer, I visited my grandmother for the first time using a video call. Before that, we only talked on the phone, but this new instrument felt helpful—like a bridge between us. She lives far away, and we need ways to stay united. At first, I worried about danger—what if the technology failed? But my sister showed me how to suit the app, and everything worked perfectly. We laughed, shared stories, and even saw each other’s faces clearly. It was amazing! Now, video calls are a general part of our society, bringing peace to families separated by distance.

Questions:
Q1: What did the writer feel about using the video call for the first time?
Correct: Excited and relieved
Distractors: Scared and confused / Bored and uninterested / Angry and frustrated
Explanation: The text says the writer initially worried about danger but then found the experience helpful and amazing, showing excitement and relief. The other options contradict the positive tone and the fact that the call worked well.
---
Q2: Why did the writer think video calls were important for their family?
Correct: To stay united despite distance
Distractors: To avoid phone calls / To learn new technology / To replace visits completely
Explanation: The text states the family need ways to stay united because the grandmother lives far away. The other options are incorrect because the writer didn’t mention avoiding phones, learning tech, or replacing visits entirely.
---
Q3: What role does the writer suggest video calls play in modern society?
Correct: A way to connect people who are far apart
Distractors: A tool only for emergencies / A replacement for letters / A source of stress

Topic: a story about a traditional festival in my country

Text:
Every year in my country, people celebrate the Mid-Autumn Festival, a typical event that brings families together. Since ancient times, this festival has been about giving thanks for the harvest. A variety of foods are prepared, but the most famous is the moon cake—a round pastry with sweet fillings. Some families also avoid eating certain foods, like insects, because they believe it brings bad luck.

During the festival, children carry lanterns shaped like animals or stars. The lanterns are often decorated with colorful paper and sometimes have a small weight at the bottom to keep them steady. In some villages, people perform traditional dances in units of five or ten. Female dancers usually wear bright red dresses, while male dancers wear blue.

Before the festival, many people do research to learn about its history. A common question people ask is: "Why do we celebrate this festival?" The answer is simple—it is a time to honor the moon, the harvest, and family bonds.

Q1: What is the main reason people celebrate the Mid-Autumn Festival?
Correct: To give thanks for the harvest and honor family bonds.
Distractors: To celebrate the new year / To worship animals / To avoid eating insects.
Explanation:
- Correct: The text states the festival is about "giving thanks for the harvest" and "honor[ing] the moon, the harvest, and family bonds."
- Distractors:
  - "To celebrate the new year" is wrong because the festival is specifically about the harvest and moon, not a new year.
  - "To worship animals" is incorrect; the text mentions lanterns shaped like animals but does not say they are worshipped.
  - "To avoid eating insects" is a detail about food customs, not the main reason for the festival.
---
Q2: What do children carry during the Mid-Autumn Festival?
Correct: Lanterns shaped like animals or stars.
Distractors: Moon cakes / Fireworks / Traditional drums.
Explanation:
- Correct: The text explicitly says children carry "lanterns shaped like animals or stars."
- Distractors:
  - "Moon cakes" are mentioned as food, not something children carry.
  - "Fireworks" are not referenced in the text.
  - "Traditional drums" are not part of the festival description for children.
---
Q3: Why do some villagers perform dances in groups of five or ten?
Correct: It is a traditional way to organize the performance.
Distractors: To avoid bad luck / To carry lanterns / To research the festival’s history.
Explanation:
- Correct: The text states dances are performed in "units of five or ten," implying this is a typical or traditional way to organize them.
- Distractors:
  - "To avoid bad luck" is incorrect; the text mentions avoiding certain foods (like insects) for luck, not dance group sizes.

Text:
My favorite place in my hometown is a small café called The Book Nook. It’s run by a kind author named Mr. Lee, who writes books in his free time. The café has cozy furniture, like soft chairs and wooden tables, and the walls are covered with bookshelves. Mr. Lee often cooks simple but delicious snacks for customers.

One day, I saw him pass by with a smile, holding a tablet. He told me he uses a special app to help him earn extra money by teaching children how to read. There’s also a local organization that supports young writers, and Mr. Lee helps them too. He says, “Anybody can learn, and I want to share my love for stories.” I admire how he spends his time helping others instead of just thinking about himself.

Questions:
Q1: What does Mr. Lee use the app for?
Correct: to teach children how to read
Distractors: to order furniture / to cook snacks / to write books
Explanation: The text states Mr. Lee uses the app to teach children reading, so the correct answer is directly mentioned. The other options (ordering furniture, cooking snacks, writing books) are unrelated activities described in the text but not linked to the app.
---
Q2: Why does Mr. Lee help young writers?
Correct: because he supports a local organization for them
Distractors: because he wants to earn more money / because he is a famous author / because he doesn’t like teaching
Explanation: The text explains that Mr. Lee helps young writers through a local organization, making this the correct answer. The other options are incorrect: earning money is a side effect of teaching, not the reason for helping writers; his fame isn’t mentioned; and he clearly enjoys teaching.
---
Q3: What does Mr. Lee say about learning?
Correct: "Anybody can learn, and I want to share my love for stories."
Distractors: "Teaching is too hard for me." / "Only smart people can write books." / "I don’t like helping others."
Explanation: The exact quote about learning is provided in the text, making it the correct answer. The distractors contradict the positive and inclusive message Mr. Lee expresses, which is clearly stated.



Topic: a story about a talent show at my school

Text:
Last week, our school had a talent show, and it was crazy! Usually, students sing or dance, but this time, things were different. My friend Leo wanted to perform magic tricks, but he had trouble with his toy rabbit—it kept disappearing! The reporter from our school newspaper laughed and said, "This is impossible!" But Leo didn’t give up. He practiced hard, and on the day, he surprised everyone. His tricks were successful, and the crowd shouted with joy. Maybe next year, we’ll have a desert island talent show—perhaps with pirates!

Questions:
Q1: What did Leo use for his magic tricks?
Correct: a toy rabbit
Distractors: a real rabbit / a magic wand / a hat
Explanation:
- The text says Leo had trouble with his toy rabbit, meaning it was a fake one, not real.
- A magic wand or hat are not mentioned—Leo’s issue was specifically with the rabbit.
- The distractors are plausible (common magic props) but incorrect because the text clearly states the problem was with the toy.
---
Q2: Why did the reporter think Leo’s tricks were impossible?
Correct: Because magic tricks with a toy rabbit seemed unrealistic.
Distractors: Because Leo was nervous / Because the rabbit was too small / Because the show was canceled
Explanation:
- The reporter said, "This is impossible!" after seeing Leo’s toy rabbit disappear, implying doubt about the trick’s realism.
- The text doesn’t mention Leo being nervous or the rabbit’s size.
- The show wasn’t canceled—it was successful, so the last option is wrong.
---
Q3: What might happen in next year’s talent show, according to the text?
Correct: There could be a desert island theme with pirates.
Distractors: Leo will perform again / The reporter will help / Students will only sing
Explanation:
- The text suggests a desert island talent show—perhaps with pirates, showing a creative future idea.
- Leo’s return or the reporter’s help isn’t mentioned.
- The last option contradicts the usual (but not exclusive) variety of acts, as the text implies more creativity next time.



Topic: a story about a surprise visit from an old friend

Text:
Last week, Mark was driving home from work when he saw his old friend, Jake, waving at him from a noisy café. Mark was surprised because he hadn’t seen Jake in years! They met for coffee, and Jake looked crazy excited. "I just moved back to town!" Jake said. They talked for hours, arguing about old times and laughing. Jake even brought a bag of nuts to share. Later, Mark noticed Jake’s sock was missing, and his pants were torn. "Don’t worry," Jake joked, "I’ll fix them less later." Their discussion ended with a promise to meet again soon.

Questions:
Q1: What did Mark see that surprised him while driving home?
Correct: His old friend Jake waving at him from a café.
Distractors: A noisy dog barking loudly / A broken car on the road / A bag of nuts on the sidewalk.
Explanation:
- The text says Mark saw Jake waving from a café, so the correct answer must mention Jake.
- "A noisy dog" is plausible (the text mentions noisy), but the focus is on Jake, not a dog.
- "A broken car" is unrelated to the story.
- "A bag of nuts" is mentioned, but it was brought by Jake after they met, not seen while driving.
---
Q2: Why did Jake look crazy excited when he met Mark?
Correct: Because he had just moved back to town.
Distractors: Because he won a big prize / Because he was arguing with someone / Because he lost his sock.
Explanation:
- The text states Jake said, "I just moved back to town!" as the reason for his excitement.
- "Won a big prize" is not mentioned.
- "Arguing with someone" is true (they argued about old times), but it wasn’t the reason for his excitement.
- "Lost his sock" is true (his sock was missing), but it’s not why he was excited.
---
Q3: What did Jake promise to do less of later?
Correct: Fix his torn pants.
Distractors: Eat nuts / Have discussions / Drive carefully.
Explanation:
- The text says, "I’ll fix them less later," referring to his torn pants.
- "Eat nuts" is plausible (they shared nuts), but the text doesn’t say he’d eat less of them.
- "Discussions" are mentioned, but the phrase is about fixing his pants, not reducing talks.
- "Drive carefully" is unrelated to the context.



Topic: a story about a time my friend helped me when I was sad

Text:
Last weekend, I was very sad because my favorite wood necklace was stolen from my store bag. I couldn’t stop crying, and my friend Leo saw me. He knew I loved animals, so he took me to the park. There, we saw a round pond where ducks were swimming. Leo said, “Let’s feed them!” We bought some bread and threw it into the water. The ducks caught the pieces happily, and I laughed for the first time in days.

Later, Leo told me about his job. He’s a designer who makes web pages for small businesses. He said, “Sometimes, life feels like a big gap between what we want and what we have.” But then he smiled and added, “But friends help us fill that gap!” His words made me feel better. Now, I know that even when a thief takes something, good people can bring back the joy.

Questions:
Q1: What made the narrator feel better in the park?
Correct: Feeding the ducks
Distractors: Buying a new necklace / Talking about the thief / Designing a website
Explanation:
- Correct: The text says Leo took the narrator to the park, where they fed ducks, and the narrator laughed for the first time. This action directly improved their mood.
- Distractors:
  - Buying a new necklace – The necklace was stolen, and there’s no mention of buying a replacement.
  - Talking about the thief – The thief is mentioned only briefly, and the focus is on the park activity, not the crime.
  - Designing a website – Leo’s job is mentioned, but it’s not what made the narrator feel better in the moment.
---
Q2: Why did Leo compare life to a “big gap”?
Correct: Because sometimes we feel a difference between our wishes and reality
Distractors: Because he was a bad designer / Because the pond was empty / Because the ducks were far away
Explanation:
- Correct: The text states Leo said, “Sometimes, life feels like a big gap between what we want and what we have.” This matches the idea of a difference between wishes and reality.
- Distractors:
  - Because he was a bad designer – Leo’s job is unrelated to his metaphor about life.
  - Because the pond was empty – The pond had ducks, so it wasn’t empty.
  - Because the ducks were far away – The ducks were close enough to feed, so distance wasn’t the issue.
---
Q3: What did Leo do to help his friend?
Correct: Took her to the park and cheered her up
Distractors: Called the police about the thief / Gave her money for a new bag / Taught her how to design websites
Explanation:
- Correct: The text describes Leo seeing the narrator sad, taking her to the park, and making her laugh by feeding ducks. This shows he cheered her up.
- Distractors:
  - Called the police about the thief – The narrator didn’t mention reporting the theft.
  - Gave her money for a new bag – There’s no mention of money or a new bag.
  - Taught her how to design websites – Leo’s job was mentioned, but he didn’t teach the narrator anything.

Wow, it was hilarious!

During the show, another group’s pet (a hamster) ran onto the table and started a little race around the room. Everyone laughed. At the end, the teacher said, "Please, next time, keep the hamster in its cage!" But we all agreed—it was the most fun project ever!

Distractors: It bit someone / It fell asleep / It escaped from the classroom.

I guess losing keys is an average problem, but finding them in a shoe is not!

I put on my shoes first, grabbed my bag, and ran as fast as I could. A kind lady even helped me with my ticket. I arrived at 7:02 AM—just two minutes late! My coach smiled and said, “You’re happily on time now!” I laughed and promised to set two alarms next time. It was a little scary, but I learned a good lesson.

Distractors: at school / in a factory / by the sea

She said, “You’re a great leader—even small things matter!” I laughed and said, “It almost fell apart, but now it’s perfect!”

Correct: a video
Distractors: a book / a friend / a document

Topic: a story about a parrot that could copy voices

Text:
In a dense forest, a clever parrot named Polly lived alone in a tall tree. Unlike other parrots, Polly could copy voices perfectly—even human speech! One day, a spider named Sam wove a golden web near her branch. "Your physical skills are amazing!" Polly said. Sam smiled and shared his secret: "I trade silk for light at night." Impressed, Polly offered to help. She copied the voices of forest animals and taught them to borrow Sam’s silk to make stronger nests. The animals worked together, showing progress in their community. Now, Polly and Sam are friends, and the forest thrives because they help themselves and each other.

Questions:
Q1: What special ability did Polly the parrot have?
Correct: She could copy voices perfectly.
Distractors: She could spin golden webs / She could glow in the dark / She could speak only in rhymes.
Explanation:
The text states Polly could copy voices perfectly, including human speech. The distractors are incorrect because:
- Golden webs are Sam the spider’s skill, not Polly’s.
- Glowing in the dark is not mentioned anywhere in the text.
- Speaking only in rhymes is not supported by the text; Polly copies voices accurately, not just rhymes.
---
Q2: Why did Sam the spider share his secret with Polly?
Correct: Because Polly admired his physical skills.
Distractors: Because Polly promised to give him gold / Because he needed help copying voices / Because Polly threatened to tell others.
Explanation:
The text says Polly complimented Sam’s skills ("Your physical skills are amazing!"), which made him share his secret. The distractors are wrong because:
- Gold is not mentioned as a condition for sharing.
- Help copying voices is reversed—Polly helps others with voices, not Sam.
- Threatening to tell others is never stated in the text.
---
Q3: How did Polly and Sam help the forest community?
Correct: Polly taught animals to borrow Sam’s silk for stronger nests.
Distractors: They built a golden bridge for animals / They scared away predators with voices / They collected light for the forest at night.
Explanation:
The text explains Polly used her voice-copying skill to teach animals to use Sam’s silk, improving their nests. The distractors are incorrect because:
- Golden bridge is not mentioned.
- Scaring predators is not the focus; the goal was cooperation.
- Collecting light is Sam’s personal trade, not a community effort.



Topic: a story about a stranger who helped me when I was lost

Text:
Last summer, I traveled to a social festival in the mountains. While exploring, I took a wrong turn and found myself lost in the wood. The sun began to set, and I felt scared. Suddenly, a stranger appeared. He was wearing a gold jacket and had a kind smile. "Don’t worry," he said. "I’ll help you." He gave me a specific map and even shared his gift—a warm sandwich—before leading me back to the stage where the festival was.

As we walked, a strong wind gave me a blow, almost knocking me over. The stranger caught me and laughed, saying, "Perhaps next time, you’ll listen to directions!" I felt so grateful. When we reached the crowd, he told me to receive his help as a lesson. I never forgot his kindness.

Questions:
Q1: What did the stranger give the narrator besides help?
Correct: a warm sandwich
Distractors: a wooden stick / a golden coin / a specific book
Explanation:
- The text states the stranger shared his gift—a warm sandwich, so this is correct.
- A wooden stick is not mentioned; the narrator was in the wood, but no stick was given.
- A golden coin is not mentioned; the stranger wore a gold jacket, but no coin was given.
- A specific book is not mentioned; the stranger gave a map, not a book.
---
Q2: Why did the stranger laugh when the wind blew the narrator?
Correct: Because he thought the narrator should listen to directions next time.
Distractors: Because he was happy to see the narrator / Because the wind was funny / Because the narrator was scared
Explanation:
- The text says the stranger laughed and said, "Perhaps next time, you’ll listen to directions!", so this is correct.
- The stranger was kind, not necessarily happy to see the narrator (no evidence of joy).
- The wind itself was not funny; the stranger’s reaction was about directions.
- The narrator was scared, but the stranger’s laugh was not because of fear—it was a playful comment.
---
Q3: What did the stranger tell the narrator to do when they reached the crowd?
Correct: Receive his help as a lesson
Distractors: Give him a golden gift / Join the social event / Find the stage alone
Explanation:
- The text says the stranger told the narrator to receive his help as a lesson, so this is correct.
- No golden gift was mentioned; the stranger wore gold, but no gift was exchanged.
- The narrator did not join the event alone; the stranger helped him reach the stage.
- The narrator was already at the stage with the stranger’s help, so finding it alone was unnecessary.



Topic: a fairy tale about a clever princess who solves puzzles to save her kingdom

Text:
Princess Lila was a teenage girl, but she was very clever. One day, the evil King Vex demanded a method to prove her kingdom’s strength. He gave her three impossible puzzles to solve in one week. Lila noticed a tip in her grandfather’s old diary: "The answer lies in the stars." She studied the sky at night and found a pattern.

The first puzzle was to walk a long distance without moving. Lila stood in one position and waited. The second was to lift a heavy stone with one finger—she used a weak rope to pull it. The third was to rate the kingdom’s happiness. She asked everyone to smile, and they all did!

King Vex was surprised and let the kingdom go free.

Questions:
Q1: What did Princess Lila find in her grandfather’s diary?
Correct: A clue about the stars
Distractors: A map of the kingdom / A secret weapon / A list of spells
Explanation:
The text states she found a tip in the diary: "The answer lies in the stars." The other options are not mentioned—no map, weapon, or spells appear. The clue directly helps her solve the puzzles, making it the correct answer.
---
Q2: How did Lila solve the puzzle about walking a long distance without moving?
Correct: She stood still in one position
Distractors: She ran very fast / She used magic / She drew a picture
Explanation:
The text explains she "stood in one position and waited." Running, magic, or drawing are not described as her method. The solution relies on her clever observation of the puzzle’s wording.
---
Q3: Why was King Vex surprised at the end?
Correct: Because Lila solved all the puzzles
Distractors: Because she fought him / Because she tricked him / Because she ran away
Explanation:
The text says King Vex "was surprised" after Lila solved the puzzles, leading him to "let the kingdom go free." Fighting, tricking, or fleeing are not mentioned—her cleverness, not conflict, resolves the situation.

Correct: To protect their forest from a burning firefly

Topic: a fairy tale about a dragon who was evil, but became kind

Text:
Long ago, in a narrow mountain village, lived a dragon named Zara. She was once evil, burning houses and scaring people. But one day, a brave pilot named Leo crashed near her cave. Zara smelled his fear, but instead of attacking, she listened. Leo told her about his tour of the world and how lonely he felt after his parents divorced. Zara realized she was also independent but unhappy. She decided to borrow Leo’s code book to learn how to communicate better. Soon, she shut her fiery heart and helped villagers, even teaching them to fly. Now, Zara is kind, and the village celebrates her every year.

Questions:
Q1: Why did Zara decide to help the villagers after meeting Leo?
Correct: She realized she was lonely and wanted to change.
Distractors: She was forced by the pilot / She wanted to borrow his book / She smelled food in the village
Explanation:
- Correct: The text says Zara felt lonely (like Leo) and wanted to change her ways after listening to him.
- Distractors:
  - "Forced by the pilot" – The text doesn’t mention force; Zara acted on her own.
  - "Wanted to borrow his book" – While she did borrow the book, this wasn’t her main reason for helping.

His father smiled and said, “Some things are not meant to be our possession, son.”

Topic: a personal story about achieving a long-term goal

Text:
When I was ten, I visited a western amusement park for the first time. The bright lights and roller coasters made me mad with excitement. Since then, I dreamed of becoming a park organizer. My parents thought it was just a childish attraction, but I knew it was my real goal.

Years passed, and I worked hard—first as a ticket host, then as a ride assistant. The job was tough, especially during summer when the sun was mild but the crowds were endless. There was always some issue to solve, like broken rides or lost children. But I never gave up.

Finally, at 28, I became the head organizer of a small park. The day I signed the contract, I felt a mix of pride and relief. My childhood dream had come true, proving that persistence pays off.

Questions:
Q1: What was the speaker’s first job in the amusement park?
Correct: host
Distractors: ride assistant / cleaner / security guard
Explanation: The text states the speaker worked as a ticket host first, not as a ride assistant (which came later), cleaner, or security guard (not mentioned).
---
Q2: Why did the speaker’s parents doubt their dream?
Correct: They thought it was just a childish attraction.
Distractors: They didn’t believe in amusement parks / They wanted the speaker to study / They feared the job was too dangerous
Explanation: The text says parents saw the dream as a "childish attraction," not a serious career. The other options are unsupported by the text.
---
Q3: What made the summer job difficult?
Correct: The endless crowds.
Distractors: The strong sun / The broken rides / The lost children
Explanation: The text mentions the sun was mild, not strong, and while broken rides and lost children were issues, the main difficulty was the crowds.
---
Q4: How did the speaker feel when they became the head organizer?
Correct: A mix of pride and relief.
Distractors: Only excitement / Fear and uncertainty / Disappointment
Explanation: The text describes the speaker’s emotions as "pride and relief," not just excitement, fear, or disappointment. The other options contradict the given details.



Topic: a story about a day in the life of an actor

Text:
Liam Carter has always dreamed of becoming an actor, but his day rarely follows a script. At 7:30 AM, he wakes up to the sound of his alarm, determined to measure his progress in the industry. His first task is a ceremony—not a royal one, but a small awards event for independent films. Though he’s nervous, Liam knows friendship with fellow actors helps him stay calm.

After the event, he meets his old friend, Emma, who studied engineering but now works as a costume designer. She jokes that his acting career is more frightening than her high-stakes projects. Liam laughs but admits he sometimes struggles to define his success—is it fame, or personal growth?

By afternoon, he attends a workshop on character development, where a famous director shares how influence shapes an actor’s career. Liam leaves inspired, realizing his journey is about more than roles—it’s about the people and experiences that shape him. Tonight, he’ll rehearse for a play about a princess, but his real story is still being written.

Questions:
Q1: What does Liam do first thing in the morning?
Correct: He checks his progress in acting.
Distractors: He attends a royal ceremony / He meets with a director / He practices engineering skills
Explanation:
- Correct: The text states Liam measures his progress at 7:30 AM, linking it to his acting career.
- Distractors:
  - "Royal ceremony" is incorrect because the ceremony is for independent films, not a royal event.
  - "Meets with a director" is wrong—he meets Emma (a costume designer) later, not a director.
  - "Engineering skills" is false; Emma studied engineering, but Liam is an actor.
---
Q2: Why does Liam find acting frightening?
Correct: Because he struggles to define his success.
Distractors: Because he has to perform in front of crowds / Because his friend Emma teases him / Because he failed an audition
Explanation:
- Correct: The text explains Liam admits he’s unsure how to define success, making the career feel daunting.
- Distractors:
  - "Perform in front of crowds" is vague and not specified in the text.
  - "Emma teases him" is true but not the reason he finds it frightening (it’s his self-doubt, not just her jokes).
  - "Failed an audition" is never mentioned.
---
Q3: How does Emma’s background influence Liam’s career?
Correct: She reminds him that acting is unpredictable, like engineering challenges.
Distractors: She offers him engineering advice for set design / She helps him audition for royal roles / She defines success for him
Explanation:
- Correct: Emma’s engineering background contrasts with acting’s unpredictability, highlighting how her perspective makes Liam reflect on his fears.
- Distractors:
  - "Engineering advice for set design" is false—she’s a costume designer, not a technical advisor.
  - "Royal roles" is incorrect; the text mentions a princess play, not royal connections.
  - "Defines success for him" is wrong—Liam himself struggles to define it, and Emma doesn’t provide an answer.
---
Q4: What does Liam realize about his career by the end of the day?
Correct: It’s shaped by people and experiences, not just roles.
Distractors: He decides to quit acting / He becomes famous overnight / He focuses only on royal-themed plays
Explanation:
- Correct: The text states his journey is about influence, friendship, and development, not just performances.
- Distractors:
  - "Quits acting" is false—he’s rehearsing a play that night.
  - "Famous overnight" is unsupported; the text doesn’t mention sudden fame.
  - "Royal-themed plays" is misleading—he’s doing a princess play, but the focus is on personal growth, not genre limits.



Topic: a story about the book that changed my life

Text:
When I was in primary school, my teacher gave me a small book with a soft fur cover. It was called "The Little Prince," and I didn’t understand much of its content at first. I pretended to read it quickly, but the story stayed with me. Years later, when I faced difficulties, I read it again and realized how much wisdom it held. The book taught me that some things are worth keeping, even if they seem simple. I felt proud that such a short story could change my life so much. Now, I remain grateful for the lessons it gave me. I even charge my e-reader every night to keep it ready for others to discover. That book was my first step toward becoming better.

Questions:
Q1: What did the narrator initially do with The Little Prince?
Correct: Pretend to read it quickly
Distractors: Read it carefully from start to finish / Ignore it completely / Ask the teacher to explain it
Explanation: The text states the narrator "pretend[ed] to read it quickly" because they didn’t understand it at first. The other options contradict this—reading carefully or ignoring it would not align with the narrator’s admission of misunderstanding, and asking the teacher isn’t mentioned.
---
Q2: Why did the narrator feel proud after reading the book again?
Correct: Because it taught them valuable life lessons
Distractors: Because it was the longest book they’d ever read / Because their teacher praised it / Because it had a beautiful fur cover
Explanation: The text explains the narrator felt "proud that such a short story could change my life so much," emphasizing its wisdom. The other options are incorrect: the book was short, no teacher praise is mentioned, and while the cover was nice, it wasn’t the reason for pride.
---
Q3: What does the narrator do every night to keep the book accessible?
Correct: Charge their e-reader
Distractors: Read it by candlelight / Lend it to friends / Write a summary in a notebook
Explanation: The text explicitly states the narrator "charge[s] my e-reader every night to keep it ready." The other options are not supported by the text and contradict this action.
---
Q4: What does the narrator believe about the book’s impact?
Correct: That it was a first step toward becoming better
Distractors: That it solved all their problems instantly / That it was only for children / That it made them forget other books
Explanation: The text says the book was "my first step toward becoming better." The other options are false: the book didn’t solve all problems at once (it took time), it wasn’t limited to children (the narrator read it as an adult), and it didn’t make them forget other books (no such claim is made).



Topic: a story about a volunteer experience

Text:
Last summer, I joined a volunteer team helping rebuild homes after an explosion destroyed a small village. The work was tough—every day, we carried heavy substances like bricks and cement, and the heat made it hard to breathe. One morning, while mixing mortar, I accidentally bit my finger, but the pain was nothing compared to the battle we faced against time.

Our team leader, a former engineer with sharp intelligence, taught us efficient techniques to maximize profit—not in money, but in the lives we improved. She explained that true profit came from helping others, not from personal gain. One day, a child asked me, "What’s a volunteer?" I struggled to find a simple definition, but she smiled and said, "Someone who gives their time, even when it’s hard."

By the end of the week, my hands were calloused, and my breath was shallow from exhaustion. But when the villagers cheered as we finished the first house, I knew the effort was worth it. The experience taught me that kindness is stronger than any explosion—and that real contact with people changes you forever.

Questions:
Q1: What was the main challenge the volunteer faced during the rebuilding work?
Correct: The extreme heat made it difficult to breathe.
Distractors: The lack of tools slowed down the progress / The villagers refused to help / The team leader was too strict
Explanation:
- Correct: The text explicitly states "the heat made it hard to breathe," linking the physical strain to breathing difficulties.
- Distractors fail because:
  - "Lack of tools" is never mentioned.
  - "Villagers refused" contradicts the cheerful ending.
  - "Team leader was strict" is unsupported; she is described as helpful and intelligent.
---
Q2: Why did the volunteer say the team’s work brought "profit"?
Correct: Because it improved people’s lives, not for personal gain.
Distractors: Because they earned money for their efforts / Because the village paid them / Because they gained fame
Explanation:
- Correct: The text clarifies "true profit came from helping others, not from personal gain," aligning with the volunteer’s values.
- Distractors fail because:
  - "Earned money" is false—they were volunteers.
  - "Village paid them" is contradicted by the context.
  - "Gained fame" is never implied.
---
Q3: What did the child’s question reveal about the volunteer?
Correct: They struggled to explain the concept of volunteering simply.
Distractors: They were embarrassed by the question / They didn’t know the definition / They ignored the child
Explanation:
- Correct: The text says "I struggled to find a simple definition," showing hesitation, not ignorance or rudeness.
- Distractors fail because:
  - "Embarrassed" is speculative; the text shows thoughtfulness.
  - "Didn’t know the definition" is too absolute—they just found it hard to simplify.
  - "Ignored the child" is false; they engaged with her.
---
Q4: How did the volunteer feel about the experience by the end?
Correct: They believed the effort was worth it despite the physical strain.
Distractors: They regretted not earning money / They wanted to quit / They felt the work was too easy
Explanation:
- Correct: The text states "I knew the effort was worth it" after the villagers’ reaction, emphasizing fulfillment.
- Distractors fail because:
  - "Regretted not earning money" contradicts their focus on helping others.
  - "Wanted to quit" is unsupported; they describe exhaustion but no desire to leave.
  - "Felt the work was too easy" is false—they describe it as tough.



Topic: a story about an artist talking about their works

Text:
Lena is a well-known artist whose work has brought benefit to many communities around the world. She started her career by painting with coal on large canvases, but her true passion lies in creating outdoors installations that connect people with nature. Her latest program, called "Earth & Art," encourages artists to use recycled materials and local customs to make their pieces.

Lena believes that art should be equally accessible to everyone, which is why she organizes free workshops in parks and schools. Her sculptures often feature strong lines that seem to pull the viewer’s eye toward the sky, symbolizing hope. She also promotes fitness through art, designing interactive installations where people can climb or walk around her creations.

Her talent has earned her recognition in the global art scene, but she remains humble, saying, "The best art is the one that inspires action, not just admiration."

Questions:
Q1: What material did Lena originally use for her paintings?
Correct: coal
Distractors: oil / watercolor / charcoal
Explanation:
- Correct: The text states Lena "started her career by painting with coal."
- oil/watercolor/charcoal: These are plausible artistic materials but are not mentioned in the text. The focus is specifically on coal as her initial medium.
---
Q2: What is the main goal of Lena’s "Earth & Art" program?
Correct: to encourage artists to use recycled materials and local customs
Distractors: to sell artworks globally / to teach fitness classes / to organize paid exhibitions
Explanation:
- Correct: The text explains the program "encourages artists to use recycled materials and local customs."
- sell artworks globally: The text mentions global recognition but not selling as the program’s goal.
- teach fitness classes: While fitness is part of her work, it’s not the program’s main focus.
- paid exhibitions: The workshops are free, so this is incorrect.
---
Q3: How does Lena’s art often make people feel?
Correct: inspired to take action
Distractors: scared or anxious / bored or indifferent / confused about the meaning
Explanation:
- Correct: She says, "The best art is the one that inspires action, not just admiration."
- scared/anxious: No emotional response suggesting fear is mentioned.
- bored/indifferent: Her work is described as interactive and engaging, not dull.
- confused: The text implies clarity in her symbolic designs (e.g., "pull the viewer’s eye toward the sky").
---
Q4: Why does Lena organize free workshops in parks and schools?
Correct: to make art equally accessible to everyone
Distractors: to attract tourists / to raise money for her projects / to compete with other artists
Explanation:
- Correct: The text states she believes art should be "equally accessible" and organizes free workshops.
- attract tourists: No mention of tourism as a goal.
- raise money: The workshops are free, so this contradicts the text.
- compete with other artists: Her focus is on accessibility and community, not competition.



Topic: a description of a movie that had a surprising ending

Text:
Last weekend, I watched a movie called The Phantom of Springfield, which seemed like an ordinary adventure at first. The story followed a young detective named Jake, who wore a red cap and solved small crimes in his hometown. One scene showed him chasing a thief through a crowded stadium, where he accidentally stepped on a toe—belonging to a mysterious man in the shadows. The man warned Jake not to dig deeper into an old legend about a poisonous flower that grew in the woods.

As the film progressed, Jake discovered the flower was imaginary, but the villain behind the theft was real. The climax took place in the same stadium, where the villain revealed his plan to punish the town by releasing a gas that would make everyone forget their past. Just as the gas spread, the heroine—Jake’s sister—encouraged the crowd to sing loudly, disrupting the villain’s scheme. The surprising ending showed the villain turning into a harmless pigeon, leaving the viewer both frightened and laughing at the absurd twist.

Questions:
Q1: What did Jake accidentally do in the stadium that made the mysterious man angry?
Correct: Step on his toe
Distractors: Steal his cap / Spill poisonous liquid / Break his imaginary flower
Explanation: The text states Jake "stepped on a toe" belonging to the mysterious man, which startled him. The other options are incorrect because the cap was worn by Jake, no liquid was spilled, and the flower was imaginary (not broken).
---
Q2: Why did the villain want to release gas in the stadium?
Correct: To make people forget their past
Distractors: To poison the crowd / To escape the town / To scare the hero
Explanation: The villain’s plan was to use gas that would cause amnesia ("make everyone forget their past"). The other options are wrong because the gas wasn’t meant to kill (just poisonous flowers were imaginary), there’s no mention of escape, and the goal wasn’t to scare Jake.
---
Q3: How did the heroine stop the villain’s plan?
Correct: By encouraging the crowd to sing loudly
Distractors: By throwing poisonous flowers at him / By calling the police / By using a spring trap
Explanation: The heroine’s action was to encourage the crowd to sing, which disrupted the gas. The other options are incorrect because no flowers were thrown, the police weren’t called, and no spring trap was mentioned.
---
Q4: What surprising transformation happened to the villain at the end?
Correct: He turned into a pigeon
Distractors: He disappeared into thin air / He became a poisonous flower / He was punished by the crowd
Explanation: The text explicitly states the villain "turned into a harmless pigeon." The other options contradict the description: he didn’t vanish, flowers were imaginary, and the crowd didn’t physically punish him.



Topic: a story about finding a magic mirror that shows the future

Text:
In a small village near the royal castle, a young carpenter named Leo lived in a solid but humble accommodation. Though he was skilled, he faced unemployment and spent his days dreaming of a better life. One evening, while exploring a deep forest, he found an old chest hidden behind a waterfall. Inside, there was a normal-looking mirror, but when Leo looked into it, he saw his future: himself wearing a golden crown, standing beside a king. At first, he thought it was a trick, but the mirror written in tiny letters: "Your destiny awaits." Leo’s heart raced with wonder—could this be real? He decided to test it by asking about a small detail: the mirror highlighted a scar on his left hand, which only his sister knew about. Now, Leo believed in the magic and wondered how to change his fate.

Correct: it looked normal
Distractors: it was too heavy to hold / it showed only animals / it was covered in dust
Explanation: The mirror was described as normal-looking, which made Leo question its magic. The other options contradict the text (no weight, animals, or dust mentioned).
---
Q3: How did the mirror prove its magic to Leo?
Correct: it highlighted a scar only his sister knew about
Distractors: it showed him a treasure map / it spoke in a royal voice / it turned into gold
Explanation: The text specifies the mirror highlighted the scar, confirming its accuracy. The other options are inventions—no treasure, royal voice, or transformation is described.
